## Hundred-and-sixth pass: the shoe sizes as built (2026-10-20; built the same day)

Sean, 2026-10-20, on the shoe sizes as built (ADR 0027), with a screenshot of US men's 2 in Midnight and Display at Fraction: "using the sizes works well, however the display in the table, there are all sorts of alignment issues clicking between rows and sizes"; "the out of range label seems to work, however I'm wondering if we have other ways we can think on it, because if I have size 11 selected and want to now see children's size but am blocked from clicking into it, this interaction may feel confusing"; on sources, Famous Footwear's chart differing from ours and a post on why a size guide misleads, "is there like a bible or official source? is ISO the best source?", two AI tools' takes shared as reference points ("may not be right"), and whether researching the top brands could make a more accurate chart, "or probably no reliable source to verify, just lots of anecdote"; on the calls: what "no setting" meant; the symbols good, the labels without the apostrophe and s ("US men", "US women", "US children", "European children", "British children"), and whether `EU` or `US` will clash down the road; and what Enter resting on the target's row meant. Context at 50 %.

| Observation                             | Plan and outcome                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| The values moved between rows and sizes | Display was at Fraction: a size row was split on a decimal point while the box's own row, and every other category's rows, are written across in Fraction, so the boundary moved with each click. Fixed: a size takes no places, but Fraction writes its half as one (`10 1/2`, its scale's own step whatever sizes are chosen) across as every row is, and Both the decimal first and the fraction after it (ConversionTable.tsx; UX-SPEC §7); the browser test sets Fraction and measures every row's right edge as one                                                |
| Out of range blocks the click           | His call, three ways, the first recommended: (a) the row says where the value falls, `> 13.5` past the scale's largest size or `< 4` below its smallest, in the muted ink, and a click takes its unit into the box with the number cleared, so a child's size can be typed; with a children's size outside its scale in the box, the rows are dashes and a click takes that unit with the number as typed, as dashes do; (b) the words as they are and the click the same; (c) the children's rows hidden while out of range, which moves rows, against the table's rule |
| Sources                                 | Answered in the reply: there is no bible; ISO is the only body and calls its own table a guideline; every other chart is a brand's or a retailer's convention, which is why they differ; the system holds ISO as the anchor and names brands as brands, later as the second value, the chart. The 2023 edition: not bought; the AI tool's claim that its free preview shows the formulas unchanged and China, Japan, and Korea as Mondopoint is a lead to read on ISO's own preview, not a source                                                                        |
| Brand charts                            | Answered: a brand's own chart maps its sizes to foot lengths, a fact for that brand, checkable against its page, not anecdote; a chart averaged across brands would be a size nobody marks. Recommended as the second value after the demand map's categories: the top brands' charts, each named as the brand's, read from the brand's site by a worker and checked blind, the `orchestrate` skill's shape (ADR 0027, decision 6)                                                                                                                                       |
| Names                                   | Built: `US men`, `US women`, `US children`, `European children`, `British children`, the spellings people type kept as aliases; the symbols as they were. `EU` and `US` will meet a clothing or ring size's symbols one day: an ambiguity across categories, which the matches show with category labels, the current category's leading, as `oz` does today                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| "No setting"                            | Explained: a switch in Settings for the US women's convention (ISO's +1 or Nike's +1.5); none until asked for, the line under the table naming the spread                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| The target                              | Explained: `us 11 to eu` reads `us 11` and keeps `eu` beside the matches; nothing acts on it yet. Recommended that Enter rest the cursor on the EU row after taking US men, so Enter again copies it; his call                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |

## Hundred-and-seventh pass: the Out of range rows, the target, "11m to EU", and the brands' charts (2026-10-20; saved before a compaction; built the same day)

Sean, 2026-10-20, on the hundred-and-sixth pass's answers, with `981005c` live: "great work on the fixes and updates"; the Out of range rows: "let's try 1 like you recommend, I will test and see how it works"; the brands' charts: ISO as the default benchmark, "but if we know how each brand compares against the ISO then we can have some solid pointers … having those deltas could help us give really accurate info to our users, cause usually users are shopping for specific brands and that's where we could really provide value … I don't think there is a good fix out there like that and we could really get an edge"; no settings for one category yet ("it would complicate our UI and UX"), later maybe a brand to select once in Shoe size, or brand against brand, but first the feasibility of gathering the data, and at the least a hint telling users the standard difference between a brand and ISO; the target: "that is genius!! yes let's do that"; and "11m to EU": "11m will be meters of course, but as I type to EU I think it becomes clear that shoe size is the right convention", so surface it first, and "where else we can implement this logic without overcomplicating". Context at 45 %; a save before the compaction.

| Observation                | Plan                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| -------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| The Out of range rows, (a) | A row whose unit's scale the foot lies beyond says where it falls, `> 13.5` past the scale's largest size or `< 4` below its smallest, the ends from the unit's `range` through `fromBase` rounded inward to the step (floor at the top, ceiling at the bottom, since the range is half a row past the table), in the muted ink: a new row tone, `beyond` (TableRow.tone; ConversionTable's TONE map, `text-ink-muted`, no new token), its value null, its text the "> 13.5". A click or Enter on it takes its unit into the box with the number cleared (TableLayout's take: `take(unit, '')`), the chip the scale's, the placeholder "Number", the rows dashes until a size is typed. With a children's size outside its scale in the box (`US C 20`), the other rows are dashes (emptyRow) and the live region says the scale's span ("US children runs 4 to 13.5"); a click on a row takes that unit with the number as typed, as dashes do. The `range` NoAnswer and its messages go. Tests: converter-state (a men's 11: the children's rows `beyond` with `> 13.5`, value null; a 100 mm foot: `< 4`; `US C 20` in the box: dashes and the announcement), the browser test (a click on the `> 13.5` row: the chip US children, the box empty, the rows dashes). Docs: UX-SPEC §6 (the Out of range sentences replaced) and §7, TESTING, ADR 0027 amended, Pick up here                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| The target acted on        | Two rules, both in one commit. (1) Enter resting the cursor on the target's row: the matches view carries the target as a unit (`search`'s `target` resolved with `resolveExact` in the matched category, else any); when Enter or a click takes the match, TableLayout rests the cursor on the target's row instead of the box's own, so Enter again copies it (Enter twice, the hundred-and-fourth pass) and the arrows go on from there; a target that names no unit of the category changes nothing. (2) The target names the category (Sean's "11m to EU"): when the whole query matches nothing and its head is matched with a target that resolves to a unit, that unit's category ranks the head's matches first, as the current category does (`prefer`), so `11m to eu` offers US men ahead of the meter, `10 oz to ml` the fluid ounce ahead of the mass ounce, `10 oz to g` the mass ounce, `5 c to f` Celsius; `m` and `w` join US men's and US women's aliases (`11m`, `8w`), which at the start stay the meter and the watt (the registry's order), and in Shoe size or with a shoe target read as the scales. Nothing else changes: the rule covers every "X to Y" or "X in Y" and no other grammar. Tests: search (`m to eu` → us-men first, `oz to ml` → fluid-ounce, `oz to g` → ounce, `m` alone → meter, `11m` in Shoe size → us-men), converter-state (the matches view's target), the browser test (`us 11 to eu`, Enter: `aria-activedescendant` the EU row; Enter again copies 44). Docs: UX-SPEC §4 (the target paragraph extended), §5 (Enter and the target's row), §8, §14 (the item built, moved into its sections); UNITS-CATALOG §0's alias decisions (`m`, `w`); ADR 0027 amended; TESTING |
| The brands' charts         | Feasibility answered in the reply (feasible and valuable, with the caveats): a research wave by the `orchestrate` skill, two workers with a list of brands each (Nike, Adidas, New Balance, Puma, Asics, Converse, Vans, Skechers, Clarks, Dr. Martens, Birkenstock, Timberland, UGG, Crocs, Reebok, Under Armour, Hoka, On, Brooks, Salomon, more as found), each brand's own size chart read from the brand's site: the foot length in cm or inches where the chart gives one, the size columns (US men's, US women's, UK, EU, JP/CM, children's where given), the page, the date, the exact figures, and whether the chart is by gender or unisex; a checker blind; the data file `docs/research/shoe-sizes-2/brands.py`, each brand's rows named as the brand's, never averaged; the deltas against ISO computed from the data, per size (the brand's foot length against ISO's for the same marked size; without foot lengths, the brand's cross-system offsets against ISO's). First product shape: a hint per brand (a unit note or the line under the table: "Nike marks EU a size above ISO; its women's are men's + 1.5"); then a brand to choose once in Shoe size, the second value in the box as Cooking's ingredient is (ADR 0027, decision 6), the table showing the brand's chart; brand against brand through the foot length where both charts give one. Cost about a million tokens for twenty brands; when: after the demand map's categories, Sean's go                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Settings for one category  | None (Sean): a brand choice, if it comes, is a second value in the box, never a setting                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

On the target: typing `11m to eu` offering a size, "so also typing like 11 us to EU, or something like 11 us to e, or even if we do not have the 'to' so 11 us eu … I don't want 11 m eu to block out a potentially better result, but in general I think 11 m eu, 11us eu, 11 us e, 11m e will likely still better lean toward shoe size unless a more popular or reliable result can fill that … we don't necessarily need to hit every single perfect nuance, but if we can expand more we should consider what makes sense and make sure we can keep it repeatable, scalable, best practice and solid UX experience".

Not done: `w` as US women's key (the watt; his call), and a target drawn in the matches (nothing is drawn; the cursor's landing says it) | Built, `31799b2`: docs/UX-SPEC.md §5                |

## Hundred-and-tenth pass: the Fraction alignment, the hidden units, and the brand's chart as the second value (2026-10-20; built the same day)

Sean, 2026-10-20, with `26fd850` live, after a compaction: "okay, overall a lot better, great work. It looks like when it's just set to Fraction, the > < and whatever other signs are still not lining up properly; please check again to make sure this is done in a best-practice way; it's a bug, right, not expected? Or am I wrong here? As I was testing I realized that for our conversions, compounds, and some units or inputs, the expected is, if it's been hidden, it will trigger the result but as hidden, right? It looks like for some it works, then for others it doesn't show the hidden unit also matches sections, for a lot of the searches or potential matches; should we have this work consistently as a best practice? I really like the brand chart as a second value for shoe size, I think that'll work best. In terms of brands we don't have good info on, is there a way we can try to get it, any suggestions there? Also for brands that do require a small clarification, are there any? Maybe in those cases we can use a hint, but ideally don't want to overdo it if it won't add much to the understanding or just confuse more."

Sean, 2026-10-20, with `28737b6` live, four screenshots of drmartens.com's US size guide (the cm and inch toggles, the How to Measure panel, the Kid's Sizing tab) and the three tables pasted as the page's text: "I'm repasting this since you did not comment on it and just want to make sure it's captured [the apparel and viewing-distance ideas], also had some deeper thoughts around it that are worth weighing and evaluating too." Then the YouTube comment on a monitor video wanting a calculator either way (monitor size or desk size to distance). "Can you evaluate too the ideas I asked you to store as notes? Not necessarily now, but the more I think about it the more I realize how there is a need out there for this and nothing exists like this: it's easy enough to manually search and convert metrics for apparel, but how amazing would it be to do apparel conversions across literally any brand, or even measurements from third-party or second-hand sellers on The RealReal, Depop, and the other things that have exploded the apparel market; users could very easily know if a size will fit, since the most reliable way is to do measurements and list those; then if users don't know their measurements but have favorite brands they can use the brand sizing as a gauge: I know I'm an S in Gap but not what I am in Nike. Sizing and measurements like that could be such a huge edge and fill a need I don't think anyone is addressing. Figure if we work that into our product, better as a separate product, better to explore in depth later as it will take a lot of similar shoe researching techniques, or if it fits well into our current path and we should incorporate it now. The things that are really going to make this converter stand out, if it's discoverable: cooking, shoe conversion, apparel conversion, compound conversions, all of this seamless; maybe we will rethink how we lay out our categories as we keep adding more. This also helps brands: shoppers understand what fits, more confidence to pull the trigger, less frustration with returns. How can we help convert in ways that require many steps and lots of labor now, and make it seamless: that's where we gain an edge. Help me place how we should tackle this: now, later, own product, a good strategy and the appropriate timing. I pasted the Doc Martens stuff as screenshots; anything else? Context is currently at 44 % remaining, so your estimate is close. Don't want to interfere with our shoe process just yet, but want to make sure we don't lose sight of this either."
